- **writing-finetune-spec-sheet**: Produces a fine-tune spec sheet that documents the seven categories an external reviewer needs to evaluate a fine-tuned model: base-checkpoint identity (org + name + revision SHA), training-data provenance and audit manifest, training recipe (objective, hyperparameters, hardware, seeds, durations), evaluation evidence (paired before/after results with effect sizes and CIs, per-segment breakdowns, eval-set hash), known limitations and failure modes, license stack across the base and the data and the resulting weights, and intended use plus out-of-scope use. Use when a fine-tune is about to be… ([rocklambros/rcs](https://github.com/rocklambros/rcs/tree/HEAD/skills/ml-datasci/writing-finetune-spec-sheet) · ★4)
- **writing-fixer**: Use when the user wants Korean prose (문서, README, 강의자료, 블로그, 슬라이드 텍스트, 주석) rewritten into plain written Korean (담백한 문어체 하다체) - clearing AI tone, colloquial and developer-slang verbs (구어체 - 돌린다, 터진다, 띄운다, 죽는다, 근데, 그냥, 안 한다), archaic or stiff wording (예스런 말, 할아버지/훈장/공문서체 - 무릇, 사뭇, 비로소, 가늠하다, 톺아보다, 곧장, ~라 할 것이다), translationese, filler section intros and outros, and decorative quotation marks - or asks to "글 다듬어", "자연스럽게", "문어체로", "AI 말투 제거", "구어체 빼", "할아버지 말투 빼", "deslop 한국어", or "writing-fixer". - **writing-flow**: A 3-step writing flow inspired by Matt Pocock. Each step is its own slash command and is "extremely demanding for the user" — you get grilled three different ways. Use whenever the user wants to write something where voice and structure matter more than speed: essays, blog posts, newsletters, opinion pieces, talks, long-form documentation, marketing copy that needs to sound human, even fiction. - **writing-flow**: Run Lars's end-to-end Substack writing flow -- interviewer (mine his take + stress-test the argument) → research-assistant (dig on any gaps) → draft → six-seat editor-panel (score to the bar) → his sign-off → the day's note. - **writing-for-adhd**: How to write text a reader with ADHD will actually read and act on — grounded in the working-memory literature (Martinussen et al. 2005 meta-analysis; Cowan's ~4-chunk capacity), cognitive load theory (Sweller; split-attention, expertise reversal), and measured reading-behaviour research (Nielsen Norman Group: concise text +58%, scannable +47%, objective language +27%, combined +124% usability; the F-shaped scanning pattern). - **writing-for-agents-zh**: 给 Agent 写文档、写 SKILL.md、写 AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md、写规则文件、让 Agent 每次跑都稳定。当用户说「怎么写一个好的 SKILL.md」「AGENTS.md 该写什么」「技能描述怎么写才会被触发」「文档太长 Agent 不看」「同一个技能每次表现不一样」「什么时候该拆成两个技能」时使用。核心概念：上下文指针（描述与规则行的措辞决定 Agent 何时去读）；两种负担（上下文负担 vs 认知负担）；信息层级（文件内步骤 &gt; 文件内参考 &gt; 披露到外部的参考）与渐进披露、同处放置、蔓延；每步的完成标准（清晰度与要求度、过早完成、后续步骤的拉力）；何时拆分（按序列、按调用方式）；引导词（用模型预训练里已有的紧凑概念锚定行为，正面表述优于禁止）；修剪（单一事实来源、环境即缓存、相关性、空操作句）。技能专属：模型调用 vs 用户调用、路由技能。也覆盖「改得更容易被正确触发」「别写成一本手册」「老是漏掉后面的步骤」这类说法。改编自 Matt Pocock 的 writing-for-agents（MIT）。 ([po-et/workbuddy-skills](https://github.com/po-et/workbuddy-skills/tree/HEAD/ported/skills/writing-for-agents-zh) · ★4)
